Obviously, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$F\in \mathcal{C}^{\infty}([0,\infty ))$\end{document}F∈C∞([0,∞)) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$F^{(pr_{0})}(0)=b_{p}$\end{document}F(pr0)(0)=bp, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0; \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$F^{(m)}(0)=0$\end{document}F(m)(0)=0 otherwise. In order to conclude, we estimate the derivatives of F of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$pr_{0}$\end{document}pr0 for arbitrary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p=0$\end{document}p=0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$t\ge 0$\end{document}t≥0, we take into account (4.1) and (4.3) in order to obtain that 4.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ |F^{(0)}(t)|\le |b_{0}|+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int _{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-t} \frac{C_{2}hM_{1}}{|1+yi|^{r_{0}+1}}\,dy\le C_{1}+ \frac{C_{2}hM_{1}}{2\pi} \int _{-\infty}^{\infty} \frac{1}{(1+y^{2})^{(r_{0}+1)/2}}\,dy, $$\end{document}|F(0)(t)|≤|b0|+12π∫−∞∞e−tC2hM1|1+yi|r0+1dy≤C1+C2hM12π∫−∞∞1(1+y2)(r0+1)/2dy, and so F is bounded. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\ge 1$\end{document}p≥1 we may write formula (4.4) evaluated at −t as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ f(-t)-\sum _{k=1}^{p}b_{k}\frac{t^{kr_{0}}}{(kr_{0})!}= \frac{1}{2\pi i}\int _{1-\infty \,i}^{1+\infty \,i}e^{-tz} \left ( \frac{\varphi (z)}{z}- \sum _{k=1}^{p} \frac{(-1)^{kr_{0}}b_{k}}{z^{kr_{0}+1}}\right )\,dz. $$\end{document}f(−t)−∑k=1pbktkr0(kr0)!=12πi∫1−∞i1+∞ie−tz(φ(z)z−∑k=1p(−1)kr0bkzkr0+1)dz. Then, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document} $$\begin{aligned} F^{(pr_{0})}(t)&=b_{p}+\left (f(-t)- \sum _{k=1}^{p}b_{k} \frac{t^{kr_{0}}}{(kr_{0})!}\right )^{(pr_{0})}(t) \\ &= b_{p}+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int _{1-\infty \,i}^{1+\infty \,i}e^{-tz} (-z)^{pr_{0}} \left (\frac{\varphi (z)}{z}- \sum _{k=1}^{p} \frac{(-1)^{kr_{0}}b_{k}}{z^{kr_{0}+1}}\right )\,dz, \end{aligned}$$ \end{document}F(pr0)(t)=bp+(f(−t)−∑k=1pbktkr0(kr0)!)(pr0)(t)=bp+12πi∫1−∞i1+∞ie−tz(−z)pr0(φ(z)z−∑k=1p(−1)kr0bkzkr0+1)dz, and we may apply (4.1), and (4.5) in order to obtain 4.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ |F^{(pr_{0})}(t)|\le C_{1}h^{p}M_{p}+\frac{C_{2}h^{p+1}M_{p+1}}{2\pi} \int _{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+y^{2})^{(r_{0}+1)/2}}\,dy. $$\end{document}|F(pr0)(t)|≤C1hpMp+C2hp+1Mp+12π∫−∞∞1(1+y2)(r0+1)/2dy. From (4.6) and (4.7), and since M satisfies (dc), we deduce that there exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C_{3},C_{4}>0$\end{document}C3,C4>0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$H>1$\end{document}H>1 such that for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0 one has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ |F^{(pr_{0})}(t)|\le C_{1}h^{p}M_{p}+C_{3}(Hh)^{p}M_{p}\le C_{4}(Hh)^{p}M_{p}, \quad t\ge 0. $$\end{document}|F(pr0)(t)|≤C1hpMp+C3(Hh)pMp≤C4(Hh)pMp,t≥0. Since h is arbitrary and H does not depend on it, we see \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$F\in \mathcal{N}_{r_{0},({\boldsymbol{M}})}([0,\infty ))$\end{document}F∈Nr0,(M)([0,∞)) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{r_{0}}(F)=\widehat{g}$\end{document}Br0(F)=gˆ, and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{r_{0}}$\end{document}Br0 is surjective. □

In a recent work, A. Debrouwere [3] characterized the surjectivity of the asymptotic Borel map in the right half-plane for regular sequences in the terms of the gamma index associated with this sequence.

Theorem 4.4

([3], Theorem 7.4)

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}$\end{document}Mˆ is a regular sequence. The following are equivalent: (i)The Borel map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\colon \mathcal{A}_{( \widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}})}(S_{1})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}B˜:A(Mˆ)(S1)→C[[z]](M) is surjective.(ii)There exists a global extension operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$U_{{\boldsymbol{M}}}:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}\to \mathcal{A}_{(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}})}(S_{1})$\end{document}UM:C[[z]](M)→A(Mˆ)(S1).(iii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>1$\end{document}γ(M)>1.

(i)The Borel map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\colon \mathcal{A}_{( \widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}})}(S_{1})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}B˜:A(Mˆ)(S1)→C[[z]](M) is surjective.

The Borel map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\colon \mathcal{A}_{( \widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}})}(S_{1})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}B˜:A(Mˆ)(S1)→C[[z]](M) is surjective.

(ii)There exists a global extension operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$U_{{\boldsymbol{M}}}:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}\to \mathcal{A}_{(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}})}(S_{1})$\end{document}UM:C[[z]](M)→A(Mˆ)(S1).

There exists a global extension operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$U_{{\boldsymbol{M}}}:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}\to \mathcal{A}_{(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}})}(S_{1})$\end{document}UM:C[[z]](M)→A(Mˆ)(S1).

(iii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>1$\end{document}γ(M)>1.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>1$\end{document}γ(M)>1.

Note that the implication \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(ii)\Rightarrow (iii)$\end{document}(ii)⇒(iii) corresponds to Theorem 4.1 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r=1$\end{document}r=1, and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(iii)\Rightarrow (i)$\end{document}(iii)⇒(i) was obtained by V. Thilliez, as already mentioned. Also, the implication \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(i)\Rightarrow (iii)$\end{document}(i)⇒(iii) is slightly weaker than our previous result applied for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r=1$\end{document}r=1. However, the full equivalence is a powerful result, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(ii)$\end{document}(ii) is deduced from any of the other two conditions.

As it occurs in the Roumieu case, see [13], this information can be taken into the case of Beurling classes in a general sector by applying general Laplace and Borel integral transforms of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha >0$\end{document}α>0, which basically arise from the classical transforms (inverse of each other) combined with ramifications of exponent α. We sketch the information needed, as the details can be found in Sects. 5.5 and 5.6 of [1].

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$0<\alpha <2$\end{document}0<α<2, to the Laplace kernel function 4.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ e_{\alpha }(z):=\frac{1}{\alpha }z^{1/\alpha }\exp (-z^{1/\alpha }), \qquad z\in S_{\alpha }, $$\end{document}eα(z):=1αz1/αexp(−z1/α),z∈Sα, there corresponds the moment function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ m_{\alpha }(\lambda ):=\int _{0}^{\infty}t^{\lambda -1}e_{\alpha }(t)dt= \Gamma (1+\alpha \lambda ), \qquad \mathrm{Re}(\lambda )\ge 0, $$\end{document}mα(λ):=∫0∞tλ−1eα(t)dt=Γ(1+αλ),Re(λ)≥0, and the Borel kernel function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ E_{\alpha }(z):=\sum _{p=0}^{\infty }\frac{z^{p}}{m_{\alpha }(p)}= \sum _{p=0}^{\infty }\frac{z^{p}}{\Gamma (1+\alpha p)},\qquad z\in \mathbb{C}, $$\end{document}Eα(z):=∑p=0∞zpmα(p)=∑p=0∞zpΓ(1+αp),z∈C, which is the classical Mittag-Leffler function of order α.

Given a function f holomorphic in a sector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S=S(d,\beta )$\end{document}S=S(d,β) (for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\beta >0$\end{document}β>0) with suitable growth, the α-Laplace transform of f in a direction τ in S is defined as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ (\mathcal{L}_{\alpha ,\tau}f)(z):=\int _{0}^{\infty (\tau )}e_{ \alpha }(u/z)f(u)\frac{du}{u},\quad |\arg (z)-\tau |< \alpha \pi /2,\ |z| \textrm{ small enough}, $$\end{document}(Lα,τf)(z):=∫0∞(τ)eα(u/z)f(u)duu,|arg(z)−τ|<απ/2,|z| small enough, where integration is along the half-line parameterized by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$t\in (0,\infty )\mapsto te^{i\tau}$\end{document}t∈(0,∞)↦teiτ. The family \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\{\mathcal{L}_{\alpha ,\tau}f\}_{\tau \textrm{\,in\,}S}$\end{document}{Lα,τf}τ in S defines, by analytic continuation, a function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{L}_{\alpha }f$\end{document}Lαf named the α-Laplace transform of f, which is holomorphic in a sectorial region (see [1] for details) bisected by d of opening \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\pi (\beta +\alpha )$\end{document}π(β+α).

Now, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S=S(d,\beta ,r):=\{z\in S(d,\beta )\colon |z|< r\}$\end{document}S=S(d,β,r):={z∈S(d,β):|z|<r} be a sector with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\beta >\alpha $\end{document}β>α, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$f:S\to \mathbb{C}$\end{document}f:S→C be holomorphic in S and continuous at 0 (that is, the limit of f at 0 exists when z tends to 0 in every proper subsector of S). For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\tau \in \mathbb{R}$\end{document}τ∈R such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$|\tau -d|<(\beta -\alpha )\pi /2$\end{document}|τ−d|<(β−α)π/2 we consider a path \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\delta _{\alpha }(\tau )$\end{document}δα(τ) in S consisting of a segment from the origin to a point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$z_{0}$\end{document}z0 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\arg (z_{0})=\tau +\alpha (\pi +\varepsilon )/2$\end{document}arg(z0)=τ+α(π+ε)/2 (for some suitably small \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\varepsilon \in (0,\pi )$\end{document}ε∈(0,π)), then the circular arc \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$|z|=|z_{0}|$\end{document}|z|=|z0| from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$z_{0}$\end{document}z0 to the point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$z_{1}$\end{document}z1 on the ray \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\arg (z)=\tau -\alpha (\pi +\varepsilon )/2$\end{document}arg(z)=τ−α(π+ε)/2 (traversed clockwise), and finally the segment from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$z_{1}$\end{document}z1 to the origin. The α-Borel transform of f in direction τ is defined as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ (\mathcal{B}_{\alpha ,\tau}f)(u):=\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\int _{\delta _{ \alpha }(\tau )}E_{\alpha }(u/z)f(z)\frac{dz}{z},\quad u\in S(\tau , \varepsilon _{0}), \quad \varepsilon _{0}\textrm{ small enough}. $$\end{document}(Bα,τf)(u):=−12πi∫δα(τ)Eα(u/z)f(z)dzz,u∈S(τ,ε0),ε0 small enough. The family \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha ,\tau}f\}_{\tau}$\end{document}{Bα,τf}τ defines the α-Borel transform of f, holomorphic in the sector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S(d,\beta -\alpha )$\end{document}S(d,β−α) and denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\alpha }f$\end{document}Bαf.

In case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha \ge 2$\end{document}α≥2, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{L}_{\alpha }f$\end{document}Lαf and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\alpha }f$\end{document}Bαf are defined by combining suitable ramification operators with the previous ones, see again [1].

The formal α-Laplace and α-Borel transforms, defined from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathbb{C}[[z]]$\end{document}C[[z]] into \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathbb{C}[[z]]$\end{document}C[[z]], are respectively given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha }\big(\sum _{p=0}^{\infty}a_{p}z^{p} \big):= \sum _{p=0}^{\infty}\Gamma (1+\alpha p)a_{p}z^{p},\qquad \widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha }\big(\sum _{p=0}^{\infty}a_{p}z^{p} \big):= \sum _{p=0}^{\infty}\frac{a_{p}}{\Gamma (1+\alpha p)}z^{p}. $$\end{document}Lˆα(∑p=0∞apzp):=∑p=0∞Γ(1+αp)apzp,Bˆα(∑p=0∞apzp):=∑p=0∞apΓ(1+αp)zp. We use the notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha }$\end{document}M⋅Γα, respectively \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha }$\end{document}M/Γα, for the sequences which are termwise product, resp. quotient, of M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha }=(\Gamma (1+\alpha p))_{p}$\end{document}Γα=(Γ(1+αp))p. According to this, note that the formal Laplace and Borel transforms, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha }$\end{document}Lˆα and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha }$\end{document}Bˆα, are topological isomorphisms between the space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}C[[z]](M) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \alpha })}$\end{document}C[[z]](M⋅Γα), respectively \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}$\end{document}C[[z]](M/Γα), for an arbitrary sequence M.

Remark 4.5

W. Balser introduced in [1] the general theory of moment summability methods, which involve generalized Laplace- and Borel-like transforms. A requirement for the kernel e in the Laplace-like transform, let us say \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$L_{e}$\end{document}Le, is that the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$z^{-1}e(z)$\end{document}z−1e(z) must be integrable at 0. This allows for a proper definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$L_{e}$\end{document}Le in order to avoid the usual shift of powers for the classical Laplace transform. The usual way to construct such kernels is by considering \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$G(1/z)$\end{document}G(1/z), where G is a flat function. In the Gevrey case, corresponding to the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha }$\end{document}Γα, the exponential function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\exp (-z^{-1/\alpha})$\end{document}exp(−z−1/α) is flat on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S_{\alpha}$\end{document}Sα. The construction of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$e_{\alpha }$\end{document}eα (see (4.8)) incorporates the factor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$z^{1/\alpha}$\end{document}z1/α to guarantee the aforementioned integrability, without affecting the kernel’s good behavior at infinity thanks to the (mg) property of the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha }$\end{document}Γα. Without (mg), it is not clear which factor should go with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$G(1/z)$\end{document}G(1/z) both to obtain good behavior at 0 and to preserve the behavior at infinity. Therefore, in our context and as indicated in Remark 3.2, we have decided to maintain the classical approach and deal with the natural shift.

The following result for the Roumieu case appeared in [13, Th. 3.5]. The fact that the constants C and c, showing up in the next items, do not depend on the value of h, makes the result valid also for the Beurling case in a straightforward way, and its proof is therefore omitted.

\usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C,c>0$\end{document}C,c>0, depending only on α, β and γ, such that for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0 and every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$f\in \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{{\boldsymbol{M}},h}(S_{\gamma})$\end{document}f∈A˜M,hu(Sγ) one has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\alpha })$\end{document}Bα:A˜M,hu(Sγ)→A˜M/Γα,chu(Sβ−α) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\alpha }\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{[{ \boldsymbol{M}}]}(S_{\gamma})\to The use of Laplace and Borel transforms of arbitrary positive order allows us to generalize Theorem 4.4 for arbitrary sectors. The idea of the proof for the Roumieu case [13, Th. 4.2] applies to the Beurling case, we include it for the sake of completeness. We note that the forthcoming result may also be deduced, under the stronger assumption of moderate growth, from those of A. Debrouwere [4] about classes with non-uniform asymptotics. We will only impose the condition (dc), and we provide an argument contained in our framework which makes Theorem 4.2 necessary.

Theorem 4.7

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}$\end{document}Mˆ is a regular sequence, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r>0$\end{document}r>0. (i)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r<\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})$\end{document}r<γ(M).

There exists a global extension operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$U_{{\boldsymbol{M}},r}:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})$\end{document}UM,r:C[[z]](M)→A˜(M)u(Sr).

The Borel map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}B˜:A˜(M)u(Sr)→C[[z]](M) is surjective.

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r< r'=1+(r'-1)$\end{document}r<r′=1+(r′−1), Theorem 4.6.(i) implies that the composition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ U_{{\boldsymbol{M}},r}:=\mathcal{L}_{r'-1}\circ \widetilde{U}\circ \widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r'-1}:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r}) $$\end{document}UM,r:=Lr′−1∘U˜∘Bˆr′−1:C[[z]](M)→A˜(M)u(Sr) is the extension operator we were looking for.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\le 1$\end{document}r≤1, consider α such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha +r>1$\end{document}α+r>1, and take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r< r'<\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})$\end{document}r<r′<γ(M). The weight sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{\alpha }$\end{document}M⋅Γα satisfies (dc) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{\alpha })= \gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})+\alpha >r'+\alpha >1$\end{document}γ(M⋅Γα)=γ(M)+α>r′+α>1. By item (a.1), there exists an extension operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ U\colon \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{ \alpha })}\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{ \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha }). $$\end{document}U:C[[z]](M⋅Γα)→A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α). Now, Theorem 4.6.(ii) implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ U_{{\boldsymbol{M}},r}:=\mathcal{B}_{\alpha }\circ U\circ \widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha }:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})} \to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r}) $$\end{document}UM,r:=Bα∘U∘Lˆα:C[[z]](M)→A˜(M)u(Sr) is the desired extension operator.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\le 1$\end{document}r≤1, consider α such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha +r>1$\end{document}α+r>1, and take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r< r'<\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})$\end{document}r<r′<γ(M). The weight sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{\alpha }$\end{document}M⋅Γα satisfies (dc) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{\alpha })= \gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})+\alpha >r'+\alpha >1$\end{document}γ(M⋅Γα)=γ(M)+α>r′+α>1. By item (a.1), there exists an extension operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ U\colon \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{ \alpha })}\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot{{ \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha }). $$\end{document}U:C[[z]](M⋅Γα)→A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α). Now, Theorem 4.6.(ii) implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ U_{{\boldsymbol{M}},r}:=\mathcal{B}_{\alpha }\circ U\circ \widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\alpha }:\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})} \to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r}) $$\end{document}UM,r:=Bα∘U∘Lˆα:C[[z]](M)→A˜(M)u(Sr) is the desired extension operator.

(iii)⇒(iv) Let us see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\leq \gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})$\end{document}r≤γ(M). We again have different cases: If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$0< r<1$\end{document}0<r<1, consider positive real numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha ,r'$\end{document}α,r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$1-\alpha < r'< r$\end{document}1−α<r′<r. By applying the Laplace transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{L}_{\alpha }\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha })$\end{document}Lα:A˜(M)u(Sr)→A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α), Theorem 4.6.(i) shows that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot { \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha })\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}, $$\end{document}A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α)→C[[z]](M⋅Γα), is surjective. Observe that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'+\alpha >1$\end{document}r′+α>1, so we deduce by restriction to the half-plane \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S_{1}$\end{document}S1 that, according to Proposition 2.8.(ii), also the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal{A}_{(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \alpha })}(S_{1})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot { \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}, $$\end{document}A(Mˆ⋅Γα)(S1)→C[[z]](M⋅Γα), is surjective. Theorem 4.4 implies then that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })>1$\end{document}γ(M⋅Γα)>1 or, equivalently by (2.5), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>1-\alpha $\end{document}γ(M)>1−α. Since α can be chosen arbitrarily while keeping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$1-\alpha < r$\end{document}1−α<r, we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})\ge r$\end{document}γ(M)≥r.If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\in \mathbb{N}$\end{document}r∈N, we know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>r$\end{document}γ(M)>r by Theorem 4.2.If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\in (1,\infty )\setminus \mathbb{N}$\end{document}r∈(1,∞)∖N, again by Theorem 4.2 we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>\lfloor r\rfloor $\end{document}γ(M)>⌊r⌋, so that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}_{1}:=\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P1:=Mˆ/Γ⌊r⌋ is such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P}_{1})>1$\end{document}γ(P1)>1 by using the properties of gamma index. Hence, by Lemma 2.7 there exists a weight sequence P such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}\approx {\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P≈M/Γ⌊r⌋, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P})=\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})-{\lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}γ(P)=γ(M)−⌊r⌋ and P will also satisfy (dc). Consider a value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lfloor r\rfloor < r'< r$\end{document}⌊r⌋<r′<r. By applying the Borel transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\lfloor r\rfloor}\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\lfloor r\rfloor})} (S_{r'- \lfloor r\rfloor})$\end{document}B⌊r⌋:A˜(M)u(Sr)→A˜(M/Γ⌊r⌋)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋), Theorem 4.6.(ii) shows that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor})} (S_{r'-\lfloor r\rfloor})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\lfloor r\rfloor})}, $$\end{document}A˜(M/Γ⌊r⌋)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋)→C[[z]](M/Γ⌊r⌋), is surjective, or equivalently, thanks to the equivalence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}\approx {\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P≈M/Γ⌊r⌋, the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{(\boldsymbol{P})} (S_{r'-\lfloor r \rfloor})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{(\boldsymbol{P})}, $$\end{document}A˜(P)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋)→C[[z]](P), is also surjective. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'-\lfloor r\rfloor \in (0,1)$\end{document}r′−⌊r⌋∈(0,1), we may invoke item (b.1) and deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P})\ge r'-\lfloor r\rfloor $\end{document}γ(P)≥r′−⌊r⌋, what amounts to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})\ge r'$\end{document}γ(M)≥r′. We conclude by making \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ tend to r. □

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$0< r<1$\end{document}0<r<1, consider positive real numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha ,r'$\end{document}α,r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$1-\alpha < r'< r$\end{document}1−α<r′<r. By applying the Laplace transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{L}_{\alpha }\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha })$\end{document}Lα:A˜(M)u(Sr)→A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α), Theorem 4.6.(i) shows that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot { \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha })\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}, $$\end{document}A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α)→C[[z]](M⋅Γα), is surjective. Observe that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'+\alpha >1$\end{document}r′+α>1, so we deduce by restriction to the half-plane \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S_{1}$\end{document}S1 that, according to Proposition 2.8.(ii), also the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal{A}_{(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \alpha })}(S_{1})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot { \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}, $$\end{document}A(Mˆ⋅Γα)(S1)→C[[z]](M⋅Γα), is surjective. Theorem 4.4 implies then that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })>1$\end{document}γ(M⋅Γα)>1 or, equivalently by (2.5), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>1-\alpha $\end{document}γ(M)>1−α. Since α can be chosen arbitrarily while keeping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$1-\alpha < r$\end{document}1−α<r, we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})\ge r$\end{document}γ(M)≥r.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$0< r<1$\end{document}0<r<1, consider positive real numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\alpha ,r'$\end{document}α,r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$1-\alpha < r'< r$\end{document}1−α<r′<r. By applying the Laplace transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{L}_{\alpha }\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha })$\end{document}Lα:A˜(M)u(Sr)→A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α), Theorem 4.6.(i) shows that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot { \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}(S_{r'+\alpha })\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}, $$\end{document}A˜(M⋅Γα)u(Sr′+α)→C[[z]](M⋅Γα), is surjective. Observe that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'+\alpha >1$\end{document}r′+α>1, so we deduce by restriction to the half-plane \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$S_{1}$\end{document}S1 that, according to Proposition 2.8.(ii), also the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal{A}_{(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \alpha })}(S_{1})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot { \boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })}, $$\end{document}A(Mˆ⋅Γα)(S1)→C[[z]](M⋅Γα), is surjective. Theorem 4.4 implies then that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}\cdot {\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\alpha })>1$\end{document}γ(M⋅Γα)>1 or, equivalently by (2.5), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>1-\alpha $\end{document}γ(M)>1−α. Since α can be chosen arbitrarily while keeping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$1-\alpha < r$\end{document}1−α<r, we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})\ge r$\end{document}γ(M)≥r.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\in \mathbb{N}$\end{document}r∈N, we know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>r$\end{document}γ(M)>r by Theorem 4.2.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\in \mathbb{N}$\end{document}r∈N, we know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>r$\end{document}γ(M)>r by Theorem 4.2.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\in (1,\infty )\setminus \mathbb{N}$\end{document}r∈(1,∞)∖N, again by Theorem 4.2 we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>\lfloor r\rfloor $\end{document}γ(M)>⌊r⌋, so that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}_{1}:=\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P1:=Mˆ/Γ⌊r⌋ is such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P}_{1})>1$\end{document}γ(P1)>1 by using the properties of gamma index. Hence, by Lemma 2.7 there exists a weight sequence P such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}\approx {\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P≈M/Γ⌊r⌋, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P})=\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})-{\lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}γ(P)=γ(M)−⌊r⌋ and P will also satisfy (dc). Consider a value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lfloor r\rfloor < r'< r$\end{document}⌊r⌋<r′<r. By applying the Borel transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\lfloor r\rfloor}\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\lfloor r\rfloor})} (S_{r'- \lfloor r\rfloor})$\end{document}B⌊r⌋:A˜(M)u(Sr)→A˜(M/Γ⌊r⌋)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋), Theorem 4.6.(ii) shows that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor})} (S_{r'-\lfloor r\rfloor})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\lfloor r\rfloor})}, $$\end{document}A˜(M/Γ⌊r⌋)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋)→C[[z]](M/Γ⌊r⌋), is surjective, or equivalently, thanks to the equivalence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}\approx {\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P≈M/Γ⌊r⌋, the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{(\boldsymbol{P})} (S_{r'-\lfloor r \rfloor})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{(\boldsymbol{P})}, $$\end{document}A˜(P)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋)→C[[z]](P), is also surjective. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'-\lfloor r\rfloor \in (0,1)$\end{document}r′−⌊r⌋∈(0,1), we may invoke item (b.1) and deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P})\ge r'-\lfloor r\rfloor $\end{document}γ(P)≥r′−⌊r⌋, what amounts to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})\ge r'$\end{document}γ(M)≥r′. We conclude by making \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ tend to r.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r\in (1,\infty )\setminus \mathbb{N}$\end{document}r∈(1,∞)∖N, again by Theorem 4.2 we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>\lfloor r\rfloor $\end{document}γ(M)>⌊r⌋, so that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}_{1}:=\widehat{{\boldsymbol{M}}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P1:=Mˆ/Γ⌊r⌋ is such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P}_{1})>1$\end{document}γ(P1)>1 by using the properties of gamma index. Hence, by Lemma 2.7 there exists a weight sequence P such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}\approx {\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P≈M/Γ⌊r⌋, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P})=\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})-{\lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}γ(P)=γ(M)−⌊r⌋ and P will also satisfy (dc). Consider a value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lfloor r\rfloor < r'< r$\end{document}⌊r⌋<r′<r. By applying the Borel transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathcal{B}_{\lfloor r\rfloor}\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\lfloor r\rfloor})} (S_{r'- \lfloor r\rfloor})$\end{document}B⌊r⌋:A˜(M)u(Sr)→A˜(M/Γ⌊r⌋)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋), Theorem 4.6.(ii) shows that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor})} (S_{r'-\lfloor r\rfloor})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{\lfloor r\rfloor})}, $$\end{document}A˜(M/Γ⌊r⌋)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋)→C[[z]](M/Γ⌊r⌋), is surjective, or equivalently, thanks to the equivalence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{P}\approx {\boldsymbol{M}}/{\boldsymbol{\Gamma }}_{ \lfloor r\rfloor}$\end{document}P≈M/Γ⌊r⌋, the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{(\boldsymbol{P})} (S_{r'-\lfloor r \rfloor})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{(\boldsymbol{P})}, $$\end{document}A˜(P)u(Sr′−⌊r⌋)→C[[z]](P), is also surjective. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'-\lfloor r\rfloor \in (0,1)$\end{document}r′−⌊r⌋∈(0,1), we may invoke item (b.1) and deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{P})\ge r'-\lfloor r\rfloor $\end{document}γ(P)≥r′−⌊r⌋, what amounts to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})\ge r'$\end{document}γ(M)≥r′. We conclude by making \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$r'$\end{document}r′ tend to r.

Surjectivity for Beurling classes under condition (sm)

We end by proving a surjectivity result for Beurling classes when their defining weight sequence satisfies the new condition (sm). The technique used by V. Thilliez in [26, Th. 3.4.1] will be followed, and we first need to recall two auxiliary results from the work of J. Chaumat and A.-M. Chollet [2].

Lemma 4.8

([2], Lemme 14)

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{L}}=(L_{p})_{p}$\end{document}L=(Lp)p be a sequence of nonnegative real numbers and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{M}}=(M_{p})_{p}$\end{document}M=(Mp)p be a sequence of positive real numbers. The following conditions are equivalent: (i)For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0, there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C(h)>0$\end{document}C(h)>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0.(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\displaystyle \lim _{p\to \infty}\left (\frac{L_{p}}{M_{p}}\right )^{1/p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞(LpMp)1/p=0.(iii)There exists a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} _{p-1} M_{p}$\end{document}Lp≤ε0ε1⋯εp−1Mp, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} ε.

(i)For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0, there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C(h)>0$\end{document}C(h)>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$L_{p}\leq C(h) h^{p} M_{p}$\end{document}Lp≤C(h)hpMp for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0.

For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0, there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C(h)>0$\end{document}C(h)>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$L_{p}\leq C(h) h^{p} M_{p}$\end{document}Lp≤C(h)hpMp for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\displaystyle \lim _{p\to \infty}\left (\frac{L_{p}}{M_{p}}\right )^{1/p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞(LpMp)1/p=0.
