Lemma 4.9

([2], Lemme 16)

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{A}=(A_{p})_{p}$\end{document}A=(Ap)p be a sequence of nonnegative real numbers such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\sum _{p=0}^{\infty} A_{p}$\end{document}∑p=0∞Ap is convergent, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{B}=(B_{p})_{p}$\end{document}B=(Bp)p and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{D}=(D_{p})_{p}$\end{document}D=(Dp)p be sequences of positive real numbers such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}B_{p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞Bp=0, and D is nonincreasing with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}D_{p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞Dp=0. Then, there exists a nondecreasing sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{E}=(E_{p})_{p}$\end{document}E=(Ep)p of positive real numbers such that: (i)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}E_{p}=\infty $\end{document}limp→∞Ep=∞.(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\sum _{p=q}^{\infty}E_{p}A_{p}\leq 8 E_{q} \sum _{p=q}^{\infty}A_{p}$\end{document}∑p=q∞EpAp≤8Eq∑p=q∞Ap, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$q\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}q∈N0.(iii)The sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{E}\cdot \boldsymbol{D}=(E_{p}D_{p})_{p}$\end{document}E⋅D=(EpDp)p is nonincreasing.(iv)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}E_{p}B_{p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞EpBp=0.

(i)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}E_{p}=\infty $\end{document}limp→∞Ep=∞.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}E_{p}=\infty $\end{document}limp→∞Ep=∞.

(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\sum _{p=q}^{\infty}E_{p}A_{p}\leq 8 E_{q} \sum _{p=q}^{\infty}A_{p}$\end{document}∑p=q∞EpAp≤8Eq∑p=q∞Ap, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$q\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}q∈N0.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\sum _{p=q}^{\infty}E_{p}A_{p}\leq 8 E_{q} \sum _{p=q}^{\infty}A_{p}$\end{document}∑p=q∞EpAp≤8Eq∑p=q∞Ap, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$q\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}q∈N0.

(iii)The sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{E}\cdot \boldsymbol{D}=(E_{p}D_{p})_{p}$\end{document}E⋅D=(EpDp)p is nonincreasing.

The sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{E}\cdot \boldsymbol{D}=(E_{p}D_{p})_{p}$\end{document}E⋅D=(EpDp)p is nonincreasing.

(iv)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}E_{p}B_{p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞EpBp=0.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\lim _{p\to \infty}E_{p}B_{p}=0$\end{document}limp→∞EpBp=0.

The next result is an adaptation of a similar result, [2, Prop. 17], in which the condition (mg) is now substituted by (sm).

For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C'(h)>0$\end{document}C′(h)>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$K_{p}\leq C'(h) h^{p} L_{p}$\end{document}Kp≤C′(h)hpLp, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0.

Proof

By Lemma 4.8, there exists a nonincreasing sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{\varepsilon }}=(\varepsilon _{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}ε=(εp)p∈N0 which tends to zero, and such that 4.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ A_{p}\leq \varepsilon _{0}\varepsilon _{1}\cdots \varepsilon _{p-1} L_{p}, \qquad p\in \mathbb{N}. $$\end{document}Ap≤ε0ε1⋯εp−1Lp,p∈N. Consider the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(u_{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}(up)p∈N0 defined as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u_{p}=\frac{1}{(p+1)\ell _{p}}, $$\end{document}up=1(p+1)ℓp, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(\ell _{p})_{\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}(ℓp)∈N0 is the sequence of quotients of L. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(u_{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}(up)p∈N0 is nonincreasing and tends to zero, and since L satisfies (snq), there exists some constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$A>0$\end{document}A>0 with 4.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \sum _{p=q}^{\infty} u_{p}\leq A (q+1)u_{q}, \qquad q\in \mathbb{N}_{0}. $$\end{document}∑p=q∞up≤A(q+1)uq,q∈N0. As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$((p+1)u_{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}((p+1)up)p∈N0 is nonincreasing and tends zero, we can apply Lemma 4.9 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ A_{p}=u_{p},\qquad B_{p}=\max \{\varepsilon _{p},(p+1)u_{p}\},\qquad D_{p}=(p+1)u_{p}, \qquad p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}. $$\end{document}Ap=up,Bp=max{εp,(p+1)up},Dp=(p+1)up,p∈N0. So, there exists a nondecreasing sequence E which tends to ∞, and such that: 4.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document} $$\begin{aligned} & \sum _{p=q}^{\infty}u_{p}E_{p}\leq 8 E_{q} \sum _{p=q}^{\infty}u_{p}, \qquad q\in \mathbb{N}_{0}, \end{aligned}$$ \end{document}∑p=q∞upEp≤8Eq∑p=q∞up,q∈N0,4.12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document} $$\begin{aligned} & \text{The sequence $((p+1)u_{p}E_{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$ is nonincreasing,} \end{aligned}$$ \end{document}The sequence ((p+1)upEp)p∈N0 is nonincreasing,4.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document} $$\begin{aligned} & \lim _{p\to \infty}\varepsilon _{p}E_{p}=0,\qquad \lim _{p\to \infty}(p+1)u_{p}E_{p}=0. \end{aligned}$$ \end{document}limp→∞εpEp=0,limp→∞(p+1)upEp=0. Let us consider the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(k_{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}(kp)p∈N0 defined as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ k_{p}=\frac{\ell _{p}}{E_{p}}=\frac{1}{(p+1)u_{p}E_{p}}, \qquad p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}. $$\end{document}kp=ℓpEp=1(p+1)upEp,p∈N0. Then, from (4.10) and (4.11) we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \sum _{p=q}^{\infty} \frac{1}{(p+1)k_{p}}=\sum _{p=q}^{\infty} u_{p}E_{p} \leq 8E_{q} \sum _{p=q}^{\infty} u_{p}\leq 8A(q+1)u_{q}E_{q}=8A \frac{1}{k_{q}},\qquad q\in \mathbb{N}_{0}. $$\end{document}∑p=q∞1(p+1)kp=∑p=q∞upEp≤8Eq∑p=q∞up≤8A(q+1)uqEq=8A1kq,q∈N0. We observe in (4.13) that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(\varepsilon _{p}E_{p})_{p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$\end{document}(εpEp)p∈N0 tends to zero and, therefore, Lemma 4.8 provides for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$t>0$\end{document}t>0 a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} We are ready for the proof of our last result.

Theorem 4.11

Let M be a weight sequence with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})>0$\end{document}γ(M)>0 and that satisfies (sm), and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$0< r<\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})$\end{document}0<r<γ(M) be given. Then, the Borel map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}B˜:A˜(M)u(Sr)→C[[z]](M) is surjective, and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$(0,\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}))\subset \widetilde{S}_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}^{u}$\end{document}(0,γ(M))⊂S˜(M)u.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widehat{f}=\sum _{p=0}^{\infty }a_{p}z^{p}\in \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({ \boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}fˆ=∑p=0∞apzp∈C[[z]](M) be given. By the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\mathbb{C}[[z]]_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}$\end{document}C[[z]](M) (see also (2.10)), for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0 there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C(h)>0$\end{document}C(h)>0 such that 4.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ |a_{p}|\le C(h)h^{p}M_{p},\quad p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}. $$\end{document}|ap|≤C(h)hpMp,p∈N0. On the one hand, by the properties of the gamma index we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}}^{1/r})=\gamma ({\boldsymbol{M}})/r>1$\end{document}γ(M1/r)=γ(M)/r>1, and Lemma 2.7 provides the existence of a weight sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{L}}=(L_{p})_{p}$\end{document}L=(Lp)p such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\widehat{{\boldsymbol{L}}})>1$\end{document}γ(Lˆ)>1 and of a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$a>0$\end{document}a>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$a^{-1}(\ell _{p}(p+1))\leq m_{p}^{1/r}\leq a(\ell _{p}(p+1))$\end{document}a−1(ℓp(p+1))≤mp1/r≤a(ℓp(p+1)) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0. In particular, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{L}})>0$\end{document}γ(L)>0, and so L satisfies (snq). Moreover, it is clear that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$a^{-p}p!L_{p}\le M_{p}^{1/r}\le a^{p}p!L_{p}$\end{document}a−pp!Lp≤Mp1/r≤app!Lp for every p, and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{M}}\approx (\widehat{{\boldsymbol{L}}})^{r}$\end{document}M≈(Lˆ)r, what implies that the classes defined by these sequences coincide. Because of the stability properties of (sm) described in Sect. 2.1, L inherits (sm) from M.

On the other hand, from (4.15) we obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{|a_{p}|^{1/r}}{p!}\le C(h)^{1/r}(ah^{1/r})^{p}L_{p},\ \ p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}, $$\end{document}|ap|1/rp!≤C(h)1/r(ah1/r)pLp,p∈N0, and so we are in a position to apply Theorem 4.10 to the sequences \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{A}=(|a_{p}|^{1/r}/p!)_{p}$\end{document}A=(|ap|1/r/p!)p and L. Hence, there exists a weight sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\boldsymbol{K}=(K_{p})_{p}$\end{document}K=(Kp)p which satisfies (snq) (i. e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma (\boldsymbol{K})>0$\end{document}γ(K)>0) and (sm), such that there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$D>0$\end{document}D>0 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$|a_{p}|^{1/r}/p! \leq D K_{p}$\end{document}|ap|1/r/p!≤DKp for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}$\end{document}p∈N0, and such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$h>0$\end{document}h>0, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$C'(h)>0$\end{document}C′(h)>0 such that 4.16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ K_{p}\leq C'(h) h^{p} L_{p},\ \ p\in \mathbb{N}_{0}. $$\end{document}Kp≤C′(h)hpLp,p∈N0. The first estimates state that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$|a_{p}|\le D^{r}(p!K_{p})^{r}$\end{document}|ap|≤Dr(p!Kp)r, and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widehat{f}\in \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{\{{\boldsymbol{N}}\}}$\end{document}fˆ∈C[[z]]{N} for the weight sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}${\boldsymbol{N}}:=(\widehat{\boldsymbol{K}})^{r}$\end{document}N:=(Kˆ)r. Again N inherits (sm) from K, and moreover \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\gamma ({\boldsymbol{N}})=r(\gamma (\boldsymbol{K})+1)>r$\end{document}γ(N)=r(γ(K)+1)>r. So, we can apply Theorem 3.3 to deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\colon \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{\{{ \boldsymbol{N}}\}}(S_{r})\to \mathbb{C}[[z]]_{\{{\boldsymbol{N}}\}}$\end{document}B˜:A˜{N}u(Sr)→C[[z]]{N} is surjective. Hence, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$f\in \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{\{{\boldsymbol{N}}\}}(S_{r})$\end{document}f∈A˜{N}u(Sr) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}(f)=\widehat{f}$\end{document}B˜(f)=fˆ. Finally, observe that from (4.16) we get \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ N_{p}=(p!K_{p})^{r}\le C'(h)^{r}(h^{r})^{p}(\widehat{L}_{p})^{r},\ \ p \in \mathbb{N}_{0}, $$\end{document}Np=(p!Kp)r≤C′(h)r(hr)p(Lˆp)r,p∈N0, and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{\{{\boldsymbol{N}}\}}(S_{r})\subset \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{((\widehat{{\boldsymbol{L}}})^{r})}(S_{r})= \widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{u}_{({\boldsymbol{M}})}(S_{r})$\end{document}A˜{N}u(Sr)⊂A˜((Lˆ)r)u(Sr)=A˜(M)u(Sr), from which the conclusion follows. □
